=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is an empty array A. For i = 1,2,\ldots,N, perform the following operation in order:

- Insert the number i into A so that it becomes the P_i-th element from the beginning.
- More precisely, replace A with the concatenation of the first P_i-1 elements of A, then i, then the remaining elements of A starting from the P_i-th element, in this order.



Output the final array A after all operations have been completed.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
P_1 P_2 \ldots P_N

Output

Let the final array be A = (A_1, A_2, \ldots, A_N).
Print A_1, A_2, \ldots, A_N in this order, separated by spaces.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 5\times 10^5
- 1 \leq P_i \leq i
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

4
1 1 2 1

Sample Output 1

4 2 3 1

The operations are performed as follows:

- Insert the number 1 so that it becomes the 1st element of A. Now, A = (1).
- Insert the number 2 so that it becomes the 1st element of A. Now, A = (2, 1).
- Insert the number 3 so that it becomes the 2nd element of A. Now, A = (2, 3, 1).
- Insert the number 4 so that it becomes the 1st element of A. Now, A = (4, 2, 3, 1).

Sample Input 2

5
1 2 3 4 5

Sample Output 2

1 2 3 4 5

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Need ensure Fenwick find kth with k maybe 1..N. Implementation:

class Fenwick:
    def __init__(self,n):
        self.n=n
        self.bit=[0]*(n+1)
        for i in range(1,n+1):
            self.bit[i]+=1
            j=i+(i&-i)
            if j<=n: self.bit[j]+=self.bit[i]
But O(N) build possible. Simpler:
bit=[0]*(N+1)
for i in range(1,N+1):
    bit[i]+=1
    j=i+(i&-i)
    if j<=n: bit[j]+=bit[i]
This initializes all ones. Or initialize bit[i] = i & -i? Fenwick tree for array of all ones: tree[i] = lowbit(i). Because sum of ones over (i-lowbit(i)+1..i) = lowbit(i). Yes. So bit[i] = i & -i. Use 1-indexed. Then find kth:

def kth(k):
    idx=0
    bitmask=1 << (n.bit_length()-1) # highest power <= n? Standard use bitmask = 1 << (n.bit_length()-1) maybe enough? Need largest power of two >=? For Fenwick binary lifting, start with highest power of two <= n. 
    while bitmask:
        t=idx+bitmask
        if t<=n and bit[t] < k:
            idx=t
            k-=bit[t]
        bitmask >>=1
    return idx+1

Need update:
def add(idx, delta):

Let's craft code:

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    N = data[0]
    P = [0] + data[1:1+N]
    bit = [0]*(N+1)
    for i in range(1, N+1):
        bit[i] = i & -i
    ans = [0]*(N+1)
    # local variables
    n = N
    max_pow = 1 << (n.bit_length() - 1)
    for i in range(N, 0, -1):
        k = P[i]
        idx = 0
        step = max_pow
        # find kth
        while step:
            nxt = idx + step
            if nxt <= n and bit[nxt] < k:
                idx = nxt
                k -= bit[nxt]
            step >>= 1
        pos = idx + 1
        ans[pos] = i
        # add -1
        j = pos
        while j <= n:
            bit[j] -= 1
            j += j & -j
    sys.stdout.write(' '.join(map(str, ans[1:])))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Let's adjust code:
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    bit = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i in range(1, N + 1):
        bit[i] = i & -i
    ans = [0] * (N + 1)
    n = N
    max_pow = 1 << (n.bit_length() - 1)
    for i in range(N, 0, -1):
        k = data[i]  # P_i
        idx = 0
        step = max_pow
        while step:
            nxt = idx + step
            if nxt <= n and bit[nxt] < k:
                idx = nxt
                k -= bit[nxt]
            step >>= 1
        pos = idx + 1
        ans[pos] = i
        j = pos
        while j <= n:
            bit[j] -= 1
            j += j & -j
    sys.stdout.write(' '.join(map(str, ans[1:])))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Let's decide final code with comments maybe:
```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]
    bit = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i in range(1, N + 1):
        bit[i] = i & -i

    ans = [0] * (N + 1)
    n = N
    max_pow = 1 << (n.bit_length() - 1)

    for i in range(n, 0, -1):
        k = data[i]
        idx = 0
        step = max_pow
        while step:
            nxt = idx + step
            if nxt <= n and bit[nxt] < k:
                idx = nxt
                k -= bit[nxt]
            step >>= 1
        pos = idx + 1
        ans[pos] = i

        j = pos
        while j <= n:
            bit[j] -= 1
            j += j & -j

    sys.stdout.write(" ".join(map(str, ans[1:])))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

```
res=0; i=500000
res += bit[500000]=32
i=499968
res += bit[499968]? lowbit? 499968 lowbit? 499968/64? 499968=64*7812? 64*7812=499968, so lowbit=64? Actually 499968 divisible by 64, not 128? 499968/128=3906, yes divisible by128? 128*3906=499968, lowbit=128? 3906 even, so lowbit=256? Let's not. Fenwick query will sum all. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "P_i" values if P_i = 1 and i=8
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return

    N = data[0]

    # Fenwick tree where 1 means "empty position"
    bit = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i in range(1, N + 1):
        bit[i] = i & -i

    ans = [0] * (N + 1)

    n = N
    max_pow = 1 << (n.bit_length() - 1)

    for i in range(n, 0, -1):
        k = data[i]  # P_i

        # Find the k-th empty position
        idx = 0
        step = max_pow
        while step:
            nxt = idx + step
            if nxt <= n and bit[nxt] < k:
                idx = nxt
                k -= bit[nxt]
            step >>= 1

        pos = idx + 1
        ans[pos] = i

        # Mark this position as occupied
        j = pos
        while j <= n:
            bit[j] -= 1
            j += j & -j

    sys.stdout.write(" ".join(map(str, ans[1:])))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
